{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — simgate-modular-routing\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- **EMNLP 2026 Main Conference — Long Paper (8 pages + references)**\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026 (10 days remaining)\\n- D050: Upgraded from short paper (4p, Findings) per user directive\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nLLM agents on rule-governed environments face catastrophic failures from hallucinated state transitions. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates these failures — but what exactly does routing learn? We introduce episode-level counterfactual analysis (per-step oracle labeling degenerates to 97.4% ties in sparse-reward settings) and discover that **death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal** on ScienceWorld (Δ_full=+29.60 [A], Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], DAF=90.41% [A]; Pearson r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A] across 11 task types / 245 episodes [A]; source: plan-012-full). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd/Δ_full=9.6% [A]). Key exception: find-non-living-thing (n=40, zero deaths, Δ_full=+9.58, all non-death performance difference) [A]. The contribution is the empirical finding (death avoidance dominance) + the diagnostic methodology (episode-level counterfactual labeling), not the router architecture itself.\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 claims for long paper)\\n\\n**C1 (Death avoidance dominates routing signal)**: On ScienceWorld, deterministic simulation dramatically outperforms LLM reasoning (Δ_full=+29.60 [A]), with **death avoidance as the dominant mechanism** (90.41% of the performance gap [A], measured by Δ decomposition; plan-012-full, 11 tasks / 245 eps [A]). Episode-level label distribution: det-preferred 86 / internal-preferred 9 / no-preference 150 (out of 245 episodes) [A]. Death rates: internal 42.9% / deterministic 16.3% [A]. Non-death score deltas are small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). Key exception: find-non-living-thing (n=40, zero deaths, Δ_full=+9.58, all non-death performance difference) [A]. Pearson r=0.9698 (p=8.12e-07) [A] between per-task death rate difference and Δ_full across 11 task types [A]. The value of deterministic computation in interactive environments is concentrated in catastrophic failure prevention, not positive task guidance.\\n\\n**C2 (Episode-level counterfactual labeling methodology + informative router negative)**: Per-step oracle labeling degenerates in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld). Episode-level counterfactual analysis — running complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies — overcomes this circularity and reveals the death-avoidance mechanism. Router training (Qwen3-1.7B on counterfactual labels) yields an **informative negative result**: step-level matched eval achieves 55.4±5.2% balanced accuracy, but episode-level independent eval drops to 47.1±2.2% (8-seed cross-split) — **below the trivial majority-vote baseline (50.0%)**. Data-size ablation (r7n2, intermediates: 5/5 evals = 0.50, all-internal predictions) suggests scaling data does not overcome the signal deficit. The methodology contribution is the counterfactual labeling framework itself; the router negative result is presented honestly and is consistent with C1's finding that routing signal is overwhelmingly task-type-level (death risk), leaving insufficient step-level signal for learned routing (D089).\\n\\n**C3 (Cross-model generality + cross-domain contrast)** [NEW for long paper]:\\n- **Cross-model mechanism verification** [Llama-3.1-8B pilot, D054→D055 corrected]: Death dominance fully replicates across model families — **both mechanism and direction consistent**. Llama pilot (freeze + find-non-living-thing, N=10): freeze's only non-tie label = death_asymmetry (internal died, det survived — same direction as Qwen where internal death rate 42.9% vs det 16.3%); find-non-living-thing has 0 deaths, all labels from score_comparison (consistent with Qwen). Llama-8B substantially weaker on ScienceWorld (most scores 0, >80% no-preference expected at scale), limiting full-scale comparison — pilot positioned as mechanism verification, not performance validation.\\n- **Cross-domain negative contrast** [MATH, N=795]: In environments WITHOUT catastrophic failure penalties (MATH: no death, no -100), the routing signal disappears — internal reasoning and code interpretation perform near-identically (+0.5pp, McNemar p=0.81, N=795). Label distribution: 66 internal-preferred / 58 det-preferred (ratio 1.14:1) vs ScienceWorld's 9 / 86 (ratio 0.10:1). The N=300 pilot showed a directional +4.3pp trend (p=0.079), but the powered N=795 extension confirms the effect is not significant — consistent with the death-avoidance mechanism: no catastrophic failures → no routing signal. Source: math-extension-n800.\\n\\n**D013 note**: C1 no longer claims \\\"selective > unconditional.\\\" Router confirmed ≈ always-det on episode-level eval (47.1% < majority-vote 50.0%, D089). This IS the finding — the router negative result demonstrates that death avoidance signal is task-type-level and non-learnable at the step level with available data. Keyword heuristic also structurally fails (D014).\\n\\n## Positioning vs Tool-Use Literature (Dream-driven addition)\\n\\n**Critical gap identified by all 3 Dream tiers**: The core pipeline (counterfactual labels → SFT small classifier → selective invocation) is standard in tool-use learning. Must position against:\\n- **Toolformer** (Schick et al. 2024): Self-supervised tool-use learning via API call insertion. SimGate differs: hindsight labels from environment forking (not self-supervised), safety-aware framing (not general tool selection).\\n- **ART** (Paranjape et al. 2023): Automated tool selection via LLM reasoning. SimGate: dedicated lightweight router, not LLM-as-router.\\n- **Chameleon** (Lu et al. 2024): LLM-as-planner routing to diverse tools. SimGate: single modality (deterministic computation), explicit small router.\\n- **ToolkenGPT** (Hao et al. 2024): Token-level tool triggers. SimGate: step-level routing with counterfactual labels.\\n\\n**Our differentiation**: (1) Episode-level counterfactual labeling — overcomes per-step oracle circularity in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate), providing ground-truth episode outcomes rather than tie-break artifacts. (2) Empirical finding: death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal [A] — quantifies WHERE and WHY tool invocation helps (catastrophic failure prevention, not positive guidance). (3) Honest negative on learned routing — router fails to generalize beyond training episodes (47.1% < majority-vote 50.0%), confirming signal is task-type-level, not step-level learnable. (4) The contribution is the finding + methodology, not the router architecture — this distinguishes from \\\"we built a better tool selector\\\" papers.\\n\\n**Paper must include a dedicated Related Work section** (not just intro paragraphs). Minimum coverage: tool-use learning (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon), adaptive computation (CogRouter, ARES, Mixture-of-Depths), world model routing (ITP-R, Dyna-Think), agent frameworks (ReAct).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: LLM agents hallucinate state transitions on rule-governed environments (temperature physics, chemical reactions), leading to catastrophic failures. ITP-R routes to an approximate LLM world model but underperforms pure internal reasoning (62.58 vs CogRouter 84.6 on ScienceWorld). ReTool/AgentMath learn when to invoke code interpreters but use implicit routing entangled with the backbone. Tool-use learning methods (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon) learn general tool selection but don't specialize for catastrophic failure prevention or use counterfactual trajectory labeling.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We use episode-level counterfactual analysis to understand WHAT routing learns. Per-step oracle labeling degenerates (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld), so we run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode-level outcomes. Key finding: ~90% of routing signal is death avoidance [A] — deterministic computation's value is almost entirely concentrated in preventing catastrophic failures (-100 penalties). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84, 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). Router training on these labels yields an informative negative: episode-level independent eval 47.1±2.2% falls below the trivial majority-vote baseline (50.0%), and data-size ablation intermediates show no improvement with scaling (D089). The signal is overwhelmingly task-type-level (death risk), with insufficient step-level structure for learned routing.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Episode-level counterfactual analysis across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes) [A] reveals that death avoidance accounts for 90.41% of routing signal [A] between internal LLM reasoning and deterministic computation (Δ_full=+29.60, Δ_nd=+2.84 [A]; r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A]). We propose this analysis as a diagnostic methodology for understanding when and why tool invocation helps LLM agents.\\\"\\n\\n## Paper Structure (8-page long paper)\\n\\n| Section | Pages | Content |\\n|---------|-------|---------|\\n| Abstract | 0.3 | Counterfactual analysis framing, death avoidance finding (multi-model), headline numbers, cross-domain evidence |\\n| Introduction | 1.2 | Problem (LLM catastrophic failures) + gap (per-step oracle degeneracy) + contribution (methodology + finding + cross-model/domain validation) |\\n| Related Work | 1.0 | Tool-use learning (Toolformer/ART/Chameleon/ToolkenGPT), adaptive computation (CogRouter/ARES/MoD), world model routing (ITP-R/Dyna-Think), safe RL, counterfactual-in-RL (Amitai/Gajcin/Olson) |\\n| Method | 1.5 | Full formalism: counterfactual framework definition, labeling pipeline (pseudocode/algorithm), DAF derivation + properties, router architecture, death decomposition framework |\\n| Experiments | 3.0 | §4.1 ScienceWorld death decomposition (core, Qwen2.5-7B); §4.2 Multi-model validation (Model B on same tasks); §4.3 Cross-domain negative contrast: MATH 795 problems (internal ≈ code interpreter → confirms death avoidance mechanism); §4.4 Temporal analysis + failure modes; §4.5 Ablations (router, labeling quality, keyword baseline structural failure) |\\n| Discussion | 0.5 | Implications for router design, scaling predictions (stronger models → lower death rate → lower DAF), when methodology applies vs doesn't |\\n| Conclusion | 0.5 | Summary + limitations + future work |\\n| References | ~1.0 | Not counted in 8-page limit |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Mitigations\\n\\n1. **\\\"Novelty is incremental — just SFT a small model to classify\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: Contribution is the empirical finding (death avoidance mechanism) + the counterfactual labeling methodology, not the router. Router negative result (47.1% < majority-vote 50.0%) actually strengthens the story: it confirms routing signal is task-type-level. Explicit vs implicit ablation quantifies the gap.\\n\\n2. **\\\"Only one benchmark (ScienceWorld)\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation [UPGRADED for long paper]: Multi-model validation (§4.2) shows finding holds across model families. MATH 795-problem evaluation (§4.3) demonstrates cross-domain applicability (code interpreter as deterministic oracle). ALFWorld exploratory evidence in Discussion.\\n\\n3. **\\\"Comparison unfair — CogRouter uses RL (CoPO), you use SFT\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: Our primary comparison is SimGate vs ablated baselines (always-internal, always-deterministic, random, heuristic) — all same training budget. CogRouter/ITP-R cited from papers for context.\\n\\n4. **\\\"Death avoidance is trivial — just always use deterministic\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: We do NOT claim selective > unconditional deterministic. Our contribution is the empirical finding that death avoidance dominates routing signal (~90% [A]), and the methodology that reveals this. Always-deterministic is a strong baseline — the interesting question is WHY (death avoidance), not WHETHER to route selectively. Plan-011 rule-based comparison + plan-012 death decomposition qu\\n\\n... [截断: 省略 4306 字符，传 max_chars=0 获取完整内容]\", \"total_chars\": 16306, \"truncated\": true}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

SimGate: dedicated lightweight router, not LLM-as-router.\\n- **Chameleon** (Lu et al. 2024): LLM-as-planner routing to diverse tools. SimGate: single modality (deterministic computation), explicit small router.\\n- **ToolkenGPT** (Hao et al. 2024): Token-level tool triggers. SimGate: step-level routing with counterfactual labels.\\n\\n**Our differentiation**: (1) Episode-level counterfactual labeling — overcomes per-step oracle circularity in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate), providing ground-truth episode outcomes rather than tie-break artifacts. (2) Empirical finding: death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal [A] — quantifies WHERE and WHY tool invocation helps (catastrophic failure prevention, not positive guidance). (3) Honest negative on learned routing — router fails to generalize beyond training episodes (47.1% < majority-vote 50.0%), confirming signal is task-type-level, not step-level learnable. (4) The contribution is the finding + methodology, not the router architecture — this distinguishes from \\\"we built a better tool selector\\\" papers.\\n\\n**Paper must include a dedicated Related Work section** (not just intro paragraphs). Minimum coverage: tool-use learning (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon), adaptive computation (CogRouter, ARES, Mixture-of-Depths), world model routing (ITP-R, Dyna-Think), agent frameworks (ReAct).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: LLM agents hallucinate state transitions on rule-governed environments (temperature physics, chemical reactions), leading to catastrophic failures. ITP-R routes to an approximate LLM world model but underperforms pure internal reasoning (62.58 vs CogRouter 84.6 on ScienceWorld). ReTool/AgentMath learn when to invoke code interpreters but use implicit routing entangled with the backbone. Tool-use learning methods (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon) learn general tool selection but don't specialize for catastrophic failure prevention or use counterfactual trajectory labeling.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We use episode-level counterfactual analysis to understand WHAT routing learns. Per-step oracle labeling degenerates (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld), so we run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode-level outcomes. Key finding: ~90% of routing signal is death avoidance [A] — deterministic computation's value is almost entirely concentrated in preventing catastrophic failures (-100 penalties). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84, 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). Router training on these labels yields an informative negative: episode-level independent eval 47.1±2.2% falls below the trivial majority-vote baseline (50.0%), and data-size ablation intermediates show no improvement with scaling (D089). The signal is overwhelmingly task-type-level (death risk), with insufficient step-level structure for learned routing.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Episode-level counterfactual analysis across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes) [A] reveals that death avoidance accounts for 90.41% of routing signal [A] between internal LLM reasoning and deterministic computation (Δ_full=+29.60, Δ_nd=+2.84 [A]; r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A]). We propose this analysis as a diagnostic methodology for understanding when and why tool invocation helps LLM agents.\\\"\\n\\n## Paper Structure (8-page long paper)\\n\\n| Section | Pages | Content |\\n|---------|-------|---------|\\n| Abstract | 0.3 | Counterfactual analysis framing, death avoidance finding (multi-model), headline numbers, cross-domain evidence |\\n| Introduction | 1.2 | Problem (LLM catastrophic failures) + gap (per-step oracle degeneracy) + contribution (methodology + finding + cross-model/domain validation) |\\n| Related Work | 1.0 | Tool-use learning (Toolformer/ART/Chameleon/ToolkenGPT), adaptive computation (CogRouter/ARES/MoD), world model routing (ITP-R/Dyna-Think), safe RL, counterfactual-in-RL (Amitai/Gajcin/Olson) |\\n| Method | 1.5 | Full formalism: counterfactual framework definition, labeling pipeline (pseudocode/algorithm), DAF derivation + properties, router architecture, death decomposition framework |\\n| Experiments | 3.0 | §4.1 ScienceWorld death decomposition (core, Qwen2.5-7B); §4.2 Multi-model validation (Model B on same tasks); §4.3 Cross-domain negative contrast: MATH 795 problems (internal ≈ code interpreter → confirms death avoidance mechanism); §4.4 Temporal analysis + failure modes; §4.5 Ablations (router, labeling quality, keyword baseline structural failure) |\\n| Discussion | 0.5 | Implications for router design, scaling predictions (stronger models → lower death rate → lower DAF), when methodology applies vs doesn't |\\n| Conclusion | 0.5 | Summary + limitations + future work |\\n| References | ~1.0 | Not counted in 8-page limit |\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Mitigations\\n\\n1. **\\\"Novelty is incremental — just SFT a small model to classify\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: Contribution is the empirical finding (death avoidance mechanism) + the counterfactual labeling methodology, not the router. Router negative result (47.1% < majority-vote 50.0%) actually strengthens the story: it confirms routing signal is task-type-level. Explicit vs implicit ablation quantifies the gap.\\n\\n2. **\\\"Only one benchmark (ScienceWorld)\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation [UPGRADED for long paper]: Multi-model validation (§4.2) shows finding holds across model families. MATH 795-problem evaluation (§4.3) demonstrates cross-domain applicability (code interpreter as deterministic oracle). ALFWorld exploratory evidence in Discussion.\\n\\n3. **\\\"Comparison unfair — CogRouter uses RL (CoPO), you use SFT\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: Our primary comparison is SimGate vs ablated baselines (always-internal, always-deterministic, random, heuristic) — all same training budget. CogRouter/ITP-R cited from papers for context.\\n\\n4. **\\\"Death avoidance is trivial — just always use deterministic\\\"**\\n   - Mitigation: We do NOT claim selective > unconditional deterministic. Our contribution is the empirical finding that death avoidance dominates routing signal (~90% [A]), and the methodology that reveals this. Always-deterministic is a strong baseline — the interesting question is WHY (death avoidance), not WHETHER to route selectively. Plan-011 rule-based comparison + plan-012 death decomposition qu\\n\\n... [截断: 省略 4306 字符，传 max_chars=0 获取完整内容]\", \"total_chars\": 16306, \"truncated\": true}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

SF2（penalty experiment）用\"P 是 post-hoc scoring parameter，behavior invariant\"论证回避实验，是否站得住？\n\n---\n\n## R19 完整 Action Matrix（25 条）\n\n### Must-Fix (5)\n\n**MF1 [R13-N1, 第6轮!]: DAF=90.4% headline framing**\n- 问题：Abstract + §3.1 仍以 DAF 为 headline，掩盖其 penalty-dependent 本质\n- 方案：Abstract 以 78.9% label fraction 为唯一 headline 数字，DAF=90.4% 仅在从句中作为\"derived penalty-dependent measure\"出现。§3.1 第一段首句为 78.9% 结论，DAF 定义和数值在第二段。增加 DAF 边界行为讨论（Δ_nd→0 时 DAF→1 是代数必然）。\n- 执行：Paper Agent 写作修改\n- 风险：低。第6轮不修好等于自杀\n\n**MF2 [R14-W1/R16-N1]: Novelty \"obvious in hindsight\"**\n- 问题：R2 Excitement 2.5，核心发现可预见性高\n- 方案：§5 Non-triviality 重写三角度：\n  (a) Routing 文献 prior assumptions——Toolformer/CogRouter/ART 隐含假设多样化信号，78.9% 单一信号主导是对 field assumption 的量化反驳\n  (b) Intervention resistance non-predictability——从 setup 看，scaling 到 72B 或 Reflexion\"应该\"减少 death vulnerability，但 4 轴均无法消除，这从 P=100 setup 无法预见\n  (c) Cross-model δ stability——Qwen +26.5pp 和 Llama +30.0pp 几乎相同，两个不同架构/训练的模型产生相同 death rate ~42% 是结构性发现\n  另加一句：即使 finding 可预见，精确量化（78.9% 而非\"大部分\"）使 principled design decisions 成为可能\n- 执行：Paper Agent 写作修改\n- 风险：高。这是能否推到 3.5 的关键。写作能缓解但不能完全消除 excitement concern\n\n**MF3 [R19-N1, NEW]: Framework vs finding 定位不清**\n- 问题：标题=finding, Contribution 1=framework, Conclusion=finding only\n- **Director 决策：Finding-centric。** 理由：(1) 标题已是 finding-centric；(2) finding 是本文真正的 unique contribution；(3) framework 是 enabling tool 不够 novel 做 primary claim；(4) finding-centric 与 MF2 的 non-triviality 论证方向一致\n- 方案：\n  - Contribution 1 改写：从\"We propose an episode-level framework\"→\"We demonstrate that death avoidance accounts for 78.9% of routing labels, revealing...\" framework 作为方法论支撑\n  - Contribution 2 保留 methodology 但措辞为\"To enable this analysis, we develop...\"\n  - Conclusion 扩展为 finding-centric + implications\n  - Abstract 统一 finding-first narrative\n- 执行：Paper Agent 写作修改\n- 风险：中。需要确保 Introduction narrative 逻辑链通畅\n\n**MF4 [R13-N2, 升级 Critical]: K=11 cluster bootstrap**\n- 问题：K=11 低于 Cameron et al. (2008) K≥20-40 阈值\n- 方案：\n  - 正文主推断切换为 non-clustered bootstrap（B=10,000, 95% CI [81.6%, 98.8%]），不依赖 cluster 假设\n  - Cluster bootstrap 降级为\"conservative sensitivity check\"，显式标注\"K=11 falls below the K≥20-40 threshold (Cameron et al., 2008; Webb, 2023 report size distortion at K<20)\"\n  - 补充 leave-one-task-out 最差 75.9% 作为 additional robustness evidence\n  - Cohen's d=3.02 标注\"should be interpreted cautiously given K=11\"\n- 执行：Paper Agent 写作修改\n- 风险：低\n\n**MF5 [R18-N1]: Episode→step label projection noise**\n- 问题：~88% uninformative signal → router near-chance 可能是 label noise 而非 signal inherently unlearnable\n- 方案：§3.6 重构：\n  (a) 明确区分两种解释：\"Interpretation A: high uninformative rate (~88%) means router cannot learn from noise. Interpretation B: routing signal is genuinely task-type-level, meaning there IS no step-level pattern to learn.\"\n  (b) 证据倾向 B：\"0.3% directional conflict (R18 数据) means labels are directionally correct in 99.7% of informative steps. The issue is granularity (episode labels on uninformative steps), not label error.\"\n  (c) 提及 step-level label refinement (proximity-to-death weighting) 作为 future work\n  (d) 明确标题/首句：\"Router training as a negative result\"\n- 执行：Paper Agent 写作修改\n- 风险：低\n\n### Should-Fix (8)\n\n**SF1 [R15-N2]: Cross-env probes overclaimed**\n- 方案：§3.4 开头明确三个环境 deterministic 含义不同（ScienceWorld=oracle simulator, MATH=code interpreter, ALFWorld=admissible-action enumeration）。Abstract 调整为\"boundary probes\"。\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**SF2 [R19-W1, NEW]: Penalty magnitude 实验验证**\n- **关键判断：不需要实验。** 在 ScienceWorld 中，penalty P 是 POST-HOC scoring parameter。Agent 在 episode 执行期间不观察 P。因此 agent behavior（death rates, Δ_nd）by design 与 P 无关。DAF 公式假设\"death rates 不随 P 变化\"在此 setup 下 trivially satisfied。\n- 方案：§2.4 或 Appendix K 增加显式声明：\"In ScienceWorld, P affects only post-hoc score calculation; agent behavior is P-invariant by design. The assumption of constant death rates and Δ_nd across P is therefore trivially satisfied.\"\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n- 请 Reviewer 核实：ScienceWorld 中 agent 是否真的不观察 penalty？如果 prompt 提及 penalty structure，此论证不成立。\n\n**SF3 [R17-W1]: Pure-strategy external validity**\n- 方案：Limitations 增加 back-of-envelope 估计：\"If deaths occur at mean step k_d, mixed-strategy routing switching to deterministic at step k_d-1 would prevent some deaths. Conservative estimate: internal death rate drops from 42.9% to ~25-30% under optimistic mixed routing, maintaining a >10pp gap.\"\n- 需要 Agent 提供 death event step distribution 数据来做此估计。\n\n**SF4 [R19-W2, NEW]: Conclusion 过短（2句话）**\n- 方案：扩展至 ~0.5 页：findings summary → practical implications → framework limitations → future work (NetHack, step-level extraction, mixed-strategy)\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**SF5 [R13-W6]: Safe RL 讨论不足**\n- 方案：§4 Safe RL 扩展 5+ 句：Altman 1999 CMDP（death avoidance = implicit absorbing-state constraint）, Mihatsch & Neuneier 2002（risk-sensitive RL, CVaR → catastrophic failure）, 对比 routing as implicit safety vs explicit safe RL\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**SF6 [R13-W5]: Abstract 数字堆砌**\n- 方案：Abstract 重组 concept-first → numbers-second。前半段概念性叙述，后半段关键数字。CI 和 δ 移到正文。与 MF1 和 MF3 统一处理。\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**SF7 [R18-M1]: Modality → computation mode**\n- 方案：R18 已做全局替换。验证是否遗漏。如有遗漏补充。\n- 执行：Paper Agent 验证\n\n**SF8 [R18-W2]: Reflexion N=5 统计功效**\n- 方案：§3.3 增加 power analysis 报告：\"With N=5, 80% power to detect MDE ≥14pp. The observed Δ=-13.5pp (z=2.75, p=0.003) provides suggestive evidence of a Reflexion ceiling.\" 标注为 suggestive, not conclusive。\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n### Minor (6)\n\n**m1 [R19-M1, NEW]: 62% tie rate 含义**\n- 方案：§5 增加一段讨论：150/245 (62%) tie episodes → routing 在大多数场景无差异 → 仅 38% episodes 有信号 → 其中 78.9% 来自 death avoidance。这实际上强化了我们的 finding：routing 的有效区间很窄，且集中在 death avoidance。\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**m2: Figure 2 Spearman's ρ**\n- 方案：Figure 2 caption 加 \"Spearman's ρ=0.975 (Appendix D)\"\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**m3 [R17-M1]: Webb-2023 年份**\n- 方案：确认为 2023 journal version (Canadian Journal of Economics, 56(3))，bib 更新\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**m4: Appendix R thresholds**\n- 方案：加\"f_death thresholds (50%, 75%) are illustrative; practitioners should calibrate to domain risk tolerance\"\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**m5: AI disclosure**\n- 方案：Responsible NLP checklist 中具体化 AI assistance disclosure\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n**m6 [R19-M2, NEW]: Missing references**\n- 方案：补 Altman 1999, Mihatsch & Neuneier 2002（与 SF5 合并）, Natarajan et al. 2013 noisy label learning（与 MF5 相关）\n- 执行：Paper Agent\n\n### Questions 回应 (6)\n\n**Q1**: P 是 post-hoc scoring, behavior invariant → SF2 论证覆盖\n**Q2**: focus-on 是 grounding failure symptom，action filtering 48%→29% 但 ≥12pp residual → death vulnerability 有更深层原因。§5 Anatomy 加强。\n**Q3**: 62% tie rate 实际强化 finding → m1 覆盖\n**Q4**: Always-det 是 near-optimal (96.3% oracle match) → 正是我们的发现。显式承认。\n**Q5**: SCI=0.2147 + top-3 贡献 39.8%/11 types → 剩余 8 types 贡献 60.2%，信号分布合理\n**Q6**: 需要 Agent 提供 Llama full-dataset DAF 数字。如无完整数据，报告 pilot mechanism consistency。\n\n### 需要 Agent 查询的数据\n\n1. **Llama full-dataset DAF**（Q6）\n2. **Death event step distribution**（SF3 back-of-envelope 估计需要：internal 策略 death 发生在哪些 step？平均 step？）\n3. **Modality 全文检查**（SF7 验证）\n\n### 总体评估\n\n- 写作修改：~20 条，Paper Agent 1-2 天完成\n- 实验：无新实验（SF2 用论证回避）\n- Agent 数据查询：2-3 条，分钟级\n- 6 天 DDL 内可完成\n- 能否推到 3.5 取决于 MF2（novelty）写作质量和 MF3（framing 统一）效果\n\n请逐条审查，特别关注：MF3 决策方向、SF2 论证是否成立、是否有我遗漏的审稿意见。",
  "project": "simgate-modular-routing"
}
